+  It splits on the column and level that results in the highest uplift gain (based on ``uplift_metric`` parameter type) in the subtree at that point. It considers all fields available from the algorithm. Note that any use of column sampling and row sampling will cause each decision to not consider all data points, and that this is on purpose to generate more robust trees. To find the best level, the histogram binning process is used to quickly compute the potential uplift gain of each possible split. The number of bins is controlled via ``nbins_cats`` for categoricals, the pair of ``nbins`` (the number of bins for the histogram to build, then split at the best point), and ``nbins_top_level`` (the minimum number of bins at the root level to use to build the histogram). This number will then be decreased by a factor of two per level.

+			          <ol class="list-plain pre-0 trailer-0"><li><a href="#ESRI_SECTION1_C30D73392D964D51A8B606128A8A6E8F">Potential applications
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:180: trailing whitespace.

docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:187: trailing whitespace.

docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:188: trailing whitespace.
+    	
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:189: trailing whitespace.
+    
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:192: trailing whitespace.

docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:193: trailing whitespace.
+<p id="GUID-967411D0-E723-446A-A78C-7E404362D5BD">Spatial data often exhibits spatial autocorrelation, in which nearby observations have similar values. Ignoring this in regression models can lead to biased estimates and incorrect inferences.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:194: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-D5F0CD44-5A35-4A2A-93A2-1D95FF3041F7">The <span purpose="gptoolref"><a class="xref xref" href="/en/pro-app/3.5/tool-reference/spatial-statistics/spatial-autoregression.htm">Spatial Autoregression</a></span> tool is designed to address these challenges by fitting a spatial regression model that explicitly accounts for spatial dependence. The tool can perform either a traditional ordinary least squares regression or one of the following global spatial regression models: the spatial lag model, spatial error model, or spatial autoregressive combined model. You can specify which model the tool will use, or the tool can determine the most appropriate model by performing a series of diagnostic tests on the dependent and explanatory variables. </p><p id="GUID-2203A608-12B9-4132-AD67-E3189F5F20D6">The objective of these regression models is to enable robust inference of regression models in the presence of spatial dependence. Using spatial regression models, you can be more confident in the estimates as well as provide estimates of the effects of space in your models.</p>
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:195: trailing whitespace.
+<section class="section1 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION1_C30D73392D964D51A8B606128A8A6E8F" purpose="section1"><h2>Potential applications
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:196: trailing whitespace.
+</h2><p id="GUID-D21917B8-E12F-4EAD-93CC-2F42178DD8A1">The <span purpose="gptoolref">Spatial Autoregression</span> tool can be used to account for spatial dependence in models in two primary ways. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:197: trailing whitespace.
+
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:198: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-233997E0-FC5D-44FE-99E2-3C6B9D2D0F8C">First, the spatial lag model is valuable for analyzing spatial spillover effects, such as the following:<ul purpose="ul" id="UL_46D5556ACEEE486EB2DDE39B38B6C103"><li purpose="li" id="LI_4819936B4900439E950EA0D61972235E">Public health and epidemiology—Assess disease or virus spread while accounting for spatial dependence.</li><li purpose="li" id="LI_2D02A216C0C642529529A4928AE31FFE">Criminology—Understand how crime clusters and spreads geographically, incorporating neighborhood effects.</li></ul></p><p id="GUID-FDEB41AD-E90E-456A-8D27-CF1AD0FC4C49">Second, the spatial error model can provide unbiased model estimates by accounting for spatial dependence in explanatory variables, such as the following:<ul purpose="ul" id="UL_49FFFF4A198845AE9C336C63DBBB6807"><li purpose="li" id="LI_C597746C615C426CA484CF10032D9813">Socioeconomic analysis—Evaluate educational attainment while controlling for spatially correlated factors in explanatory variables.</li><li purpose="li" id="LI_5F99239F9B584758AA6E5FF7705E0A81">Housing prices—Control for unmeasured spatial factors affecting property values, providing clearer insight into key model variables.</li></ul></p></section><section class="section1 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION1_A63D4D2A15AE491E86CB65C168E9E8F1" purpose="section1"><h2>Model types</h2><p id="GUID-050F779F-A33C-4485-94C1-DA0C985CF5ED">The <span purpose="gptoolref">Spatial Autoregression</span> tool can estimate three possible global spatial regression models, each of which account for spatial dependence in different ways. <a class="xref xref" href="/en/pro-app/3.5/tool-reference/spatial-statistics/ordinary-least-squares.htm">Ordinary least squares</a> regression is performed when none of the three spatial regression models are determined to be suitable based on various diagnostics.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:199: trailing whitespace.
+</p><section class="section2 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION2_946651C95FC9462BAFA9B731DF48BC31" purpose="section2"><h3>Spatial error model</h3><p id="GUID-5064FC8C-25DD-4106-A17D-2875B8DE23E6">The spatial error model (SEM) is designed to address situations in which there is spatial autocorrelation in the residuals of a regression model. For SEM, spatial dependence is viewed as a nuisance parameter. A nuisance parameter is one that must be accounted for in order to ensure that appropriate inferences are made. The SEM model is defined by the following formula:
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:200: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-DF69D8B5-183E-4311-8284-B073861FAB32"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="SEM equation" title="SEM equation" src="GUID-0D33E5F7-46B5-42BE-BBCE-6DFB2382FE30-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-75D6E417-BAB3-48DF-AB54-E08FB9F42DCF">It is similar to the ordinary least squares regression formula, in which a dependent variable (<span class="usertext">y</span>) is predicted by a set of explanatory variable (<span class="usertext">x</span>) and coefficients (<span class="usertext">β</span>). However, the residual term (<span class="usertext">u</span>) is modeled by a different regression equation. This second regression predicts the residual using a spatial autoregressive parameter <span class="usertext">λ</span> (lambda) and a spatial weights matrix (<span class="usertext">W</span>), along with its own residual term (ε). The lambda parameter quantifies the strength the spatial dependence in the error term and measures how much one location’s error term influences the error terms of its neighbors.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:201: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-F310D3F7-4D7D-467F-96ED-6E25B954D44E">The SEM works by filtering out spatial autocorrelation from each of the variables in the model and performing a regression on the spatially filtered variables. As a result, the coefficient estimates are not as affected by the spatial autocorrelation in each variable.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:202: trailing whitespace.
+</p></section><section class="section2 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION2_6B02A9B5265E431182147677A8D0174E" purpose="section2"><h3>Spatial lag model</h3><p id="GUID-3BFA154B-56B0-4B2C-B9E8-BC78C90D5ECB">Unlike the SEM, which views spatial dependence as a nuisance, the spatial lag model (SLM) incorporates the spatial dependence as an explanatory variable. The spatial lag model is used when the dependent variable has a large amount of spatial autocorrelation and exhibits a spatial spillover effect (meaning that changes in one area elicit changes in neighboring areas). The SLM model is defined by the equation:
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:203: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-25449BE8-685A-4227-8BBA-68E749A808AE"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="SLM equation" title="SLM equation" src="GUID-58BEFEB8-FA78-4033-8178-8F1DDF784E99-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-547B1B7D-C85D-4D18-B756-B49428CA676D">The dependent variable is predicted by the explanatory variables as well as its own spatial lag (<i>W<sub>y</sub></i>). The spatial autoregressive parameter <span class="usertext">ρ</span> (rho) measures the strength of the influence that a location’s neighbors exert on the value of the dependent variable (<span class="usertext">y</span>). Larger estimated values of the <span class="usertext">ρ</span> parameter suggest a diffusion process in which values at one location affect the values at neighboring locations. In turn, the neighbors can affect the original location, causing a feedback loop.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:204: trailing whitespace.
+</p></section><section class="section2 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION2_EED5F2FA52BB446EBF2308948863DCD2" purpose="section2"><h3>Spatial autoregressive combined model</h3><p id="GUID-143FB314-0DCE-420D-A84A-229DD1B4E443">The spatial autoregressive combined model (SAC) includes the spatial autoregressive parameters <span class="usertext">λ</span> and <span class="usertext">ρ</span> from the spatial error and spatial lag models, respectively. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:205: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-3B18795B-1065-4B4A-8522-56BCE8AF5341"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="SAC equation" title="SAC equation" src="GUID-2A3C04E2-CCC5-48B0-9B03-D5B81F6F6E1B-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-5CF28A39-820C-434D-A9FC-39D43873D7F9">In this case, the spatial dependence of the error term as well as in the spatial lag of the dependent variable are modeled. The SAC model can be used to identify spatial spillover effects in the dependent variable while also addressing the spatial dependence in the error term.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:206: trailing whitespace.
+</p></section></section><section class="section1 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION1_DFC61ED3F77040FEBB168A2CFC74D0CC" purpose="section1"><h2>Choosing the appropriate model</h2><p id="GUID-76CCA130-A446-4A6D-99BD-29F44CA7ABB8">By default, the tool will select the model that is most appropriate based on a series of statistical tests called a Lagrange Multiplier (LM) test (also known as a Rao’s score). The selection process is primarily based on the workflow described by Anselin and Rey (2014). 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:207: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-45236F3B-7A28-4CD2-B7CB-19695D569A9A">The decision criteria for selecting the model is displayed in the following flow chart:</p><p id="GUID-0E294569-4086-47A2-BD9F-491DD2F2265E"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Model selection flow chart" title="Model selection flow chart" src="GUID-EBB3EBEA-0252-425A-893C-58AA2853D338-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-4F63081C-CA1F-4959-911F-C000CAB2D310">First, the LM test for the spatial lag (LM Lag) and for the spatial error (LM Error) models are performed. If neither tests are statistically significant (p-value greater than 0.05), a spatial model is not necessary, and an OLS model is selected. If only one of the tests is significant, the corresponding model is selected. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:208: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-4DFB6F96-D27B-463C-8071-23E575BCDBE3">If both the LM Lag and LM Error tests are significant, their robust counterparts are performed. These are the Robust LM Lag and the Robust LM Error tests, which are slightly more stringent forms of the test. If only one of the tests is significant, the corresponding model is selected. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:209: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-7F0708E4-391B-42EC-A79A-2F72F18645FD">If both of robust tests are significant, an LM test for the SAC model is performed. In the case that all three tests are significant, the model with the largest test statistic is chosen.</p><p id="GUID-405DD260-A7BE-4DF9-AA0D-C1D6ACB496C2">In the rare case that both the LM Lag and LM Error tests are significant but neither of the robust tests are significant, the SAC model is chosen.</p><p id="GUID-58D93EF0-28E4-464E-86C1-64392F3BBF1B">It is important to note that the LM tests are a data-driven approach to choosing a model. They do not guarantee a good model or fit. Review the diagnostics and consider the theoretical assumptions of the model.</p></section><section class="section1 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION1_8CAD11CF48D04268A07098A40D2123B8" purpose="section1"><h2>Tool outputs</h2><p id="GUID-510D5515-81B7-48CA-B3E7-FAEB4F34DA59">The primary output of the tool is a number of tables in the geoprocessing messages as well as an output feature class and a chart visualizing the residuals of the model.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:210: trailing whitespace.
+</p><section class="section2 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION2_C8B3708E282F4FA58CC0CEDDBAFAE4CB" purpose="section2"><h3>Output features</h3><p id="GUID-7E7CB0D3-0214-4757-80F5-C7208E2C2067">The output feature class of the tool will contain fields of the dependent variable, explanatory variables, the predicted value of the dependent variable, residual and standardized residual, the spatial lag of the residual, and the number of neighbors of each feature. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:211: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-A44E5954-8387-4B09-BBED-FC8FB2B87C31"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Attribute table of output features" title="Attribute table of output features" src="GUID-DE6468C4-19C1-48BD-9FF2-5C948F0FA94D-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-208EBD71-BC4C-4F34-B3E1-07B88F40A394">When the layer is added to a map, the features will be shaded by their standardized residuals. Visualizing the standardized residuals can assist in identifying any patterns of clustering in the error term.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:212: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-F0D3268B-9824-45CF-B842-E6F32813812B"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Output layer and symbology" title="Output layer and symbology" src="GUID-97144B43-6C91-45CD-8FD5-55C14196D0EC-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-7E338B7A-8275-4894-8056-F7A86556D27F">The residuals are symbolized from a deep purple to a dark green. Locations symbolized with green have a positive residual meaning that the model overestimated the value. Similarly, locations with a purple color have a negative standardized residual. Negative residuals indicate a location that is underestimated.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:213: trailing whitespace.
+</p></section><section class="section2 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION2_10EE02417D574F1781FC99AF81F98340" purpose="section2"><h3>Moran's Scatter Plot of Residuals</h3><p id="GUID-57AD6F18-3874-4BA4-AF03-519107225BD5">The output layer contains a scatter plot chart displaying the residuals plotted against their spatial lag. The x-axis displays the standardized residual, and the y-axis displays the spatial lag of the standardized residual. This type of chart is referred to as a Moran's scatter plot.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:214: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-2A11901C-B7DC-400E-8AB3-BE853D6CD61B"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Moran's Scatter Plot of Residuals" title="Moran's Scatter Plot of Residuals" src="GUID-3B0F4047-6B11-43C6-ABEF-3E13A81BC48A-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-2254127C-20D7-4C0A-96BF-07F3745A3252">The chart can be split into four quadrants around 0 on the x and y axes. Values in the top-right and lower-left quadrants exhibit positive spatial autocorrelation. These are locations that have similar values as their neighbors: positive and negative values respectively. The top-left and bottom-right quadrants are locations that exhibit negative spatial autocorrelation. These are locations that have high values surrounded by low-values (and vice versa).
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:215: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-C16AE983-0627-4AE9-AC7B-93342035943B">When the residuals are evenly distributed across the four quadrants, it indicates that there is no discernible spatial autocorrelation. This type of pattern is expected when the regression model performed well and the majority of spatial autocorrelation has been accounted for.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:216: trailing whitespace.
+</p></section><section class="section2 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION2_D032F8867E2C4B2D930386A74BF5A721" purpose="section2"><h3>Geoprocessing messages</h3><p id="GUID-D1CEBD1D-1165-45E0-BC0E-DFCBA7E9CE0E">The tool provides a number of tables in the geoprocessing messages that provide insight into the how each model is estimated:
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:217: trailing whitespace.
+<ul purpose="ul" id="UL_13F98AB318F249C5B10F88B3D6E0F206"><li purpose="li" id="LI_D6D63CE61A924806AFF481B6329223F4">Neighborhood and Spatial Weights Summary</li><li purpose="li" id="LI_2D72262CCCB9491787DC0B9A23046E6F">LM Test Results</li><li purpose="li" id="LI_15F963CCD11C419DABEA5BDD6B8602BB">Summary of Model Results</li><li purpose="li" id="LI_B1EA8B4B09A845D5BBD28B6243C0B68B">Model Diagnostics</li></ul></p><p id="GUID-EB4AE4FD-00B9-496D-8E1D-03A9806A2053">In some cases, the following message tables will also be displayed:<ul purpose="ul" id="UL_DD40F765E73641738C5BC32D7B0D8B20"><li purpose="li" id="LI_36D321170F6F4053A94A4D648B824288">Coefficient Effects Summary</li><li purpose="li" id="LI_3ACF62CF6B9441CEA1AAE6001637826F">Coincident Point Report</li></ul></p><p id="GUID-EBE7443F-7D45-46EB-A667-F73A5D1BF72C">Each table is described in the following sections.</p><section class="section3 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION3_694990E465A347E099879D5DB37FA186" purpose="section3"><h4>Neighborhood and Spatial Weights Summary</h4><p id="GUID-73FA1079-E83C-4415-9318-2AFB0068B236">The SEM, SLM, and SAC models require a spatial weights matrix, which can heavily influence the model results. The Neighborhood and Spatial Weights Summary table provides insight into the spatial weights matrix used to fit the model. It reports the neighborhood type, weighting scheme, spatial connectivity, average neighborhood size, minimum neighborhood size, and maximum neighborhood size. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:218: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-59CE96C7-4EBB-4D89-9BD9-BA2CA58C8529"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Neighborhood and Spatial Weights Summary message table" title="Neighborhood and Spatial Weights Summary message table" src="GUID-676ADE98-AEA9-4391-A36F-1602A4AA27D7-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-7A2A7B73-BC78-4F6E-96A3-5507E35E0A56">It is important to note that the tool will not estimate a model if the spatial weights matrix is too connected. The percentage of spatial connectivity is approximately the average number of neighbors for each feature, as a percent of the total number of features. For example, with 500 features and spatial connectivity equal to 0.1, each feature will have approximately 50 neighbors on average. If the spatial weights matrix has a connectivity of 30 percent or greater, the model results will become biased (Smith, 2009). In this case, the tool will return an error. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:219: trailing whitespace.
+</p></section><section class="section3 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION3_D302FD49F08B4D5597D5A6F6300A38E1" purpose="section3"><h4>LM Test Results</h4><p id="GUID-8A27A374-1216-498D-9870-D5797AA64D80">The LM Test Results table reports the Lagrange Multiplier diagnostics for each of the tests. The table also displays the model type that would be selected based on the flow chart in the <a class="xref xref" href="/en/pro-app/3.5/tool-reference/spatial-statistics/how-spatial-autoregression-works.htm#ESRI_SECTION1_DFC61ED3F77040FEBB168A2CFC74D0CC">Choosing the appropriate model</a> section above.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:220: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-530A1920-0AB3-4D9B-8B92-A69C76BD2961"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="LM Test Results message table" title="LM Test Results message table" src="GUID-799DE340-5A90-437F-9C04-7C373A3E92A3-web.png" loading="lazy"></p></section><section class="section3 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION3_863C347B790E484BAB93F18482EDD68C" purpose="section3"><h4>Coincident Point Report</h4><p id="GUID-7F3D6C16-08F6-472B-905A-58D138108EFA">Coincident points (points with the same coordinates) can cause various issues in spatial regression, such as creating weights equal to zero for all neighbors. If coincident points are present in your input features, a Coincident Point Report will be displayed that reports the total features, the number of unique locations, as well as the minimum, maximum, and average number of coincident points for all features. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:221: trailing whitespace.
+Additionally, warnings and errors caused by the coincident points may be displayed.</p><p id="GUID-2408B0CB-665D-4ABE-A374-F0B7901995BD"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Coincident Point Report message table" title="Coincident Point Report message table" src="GUID-A522B599-4683-4099-801F-CD993B544906-web.png" loading="lazy"></p></section><section class="section3 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION3_F4925BBCFEFC48C0A5E5AF476241B931" purpose="section3"><h4>Model Diagnostics</h4><p id="GUID-9466120F-F6DF-4DE2-ABF8-F3B81D44FB5D">The Model Diagnostics table displays important diagnostics, such as the dependent variable, the number of features, degrees of freedom, as well as which model was used. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:222: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-63B38424-E1F7-4018-86F3-834CDC537F6A"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Model Diagnostics message table" title="Model Diagnostics message table" src="GUID-231E1823-E94E-4E13-8E48-372865814D1F-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-5A214D90-1324-4FC8-B9FA-D96E81CEB402">If an OLS model is estimated, the adjusted R-squared is displayed in the table. However, for all spatial models, a pseudo R-squared is displayed instead. For the SLM and the SAC models, a spatial pseudo R-squared is also displayed. These are discussed below. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:223: trailing whitespace.
+
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:224: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-0BEABCA1-AA0C-49F8-AA6D-9C7E8F158FF2">Additionally, the Jarque-Bera statistic is also reported. If the statistic is significant, it indicates that the model’s residuals are not normally distributed. While the models are estimated using methods robust to non-normality, the test may indicate a model misspecification or the presence of outliers. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:225: trailing whitespace.
+</p></section></section></section><section class="section1 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION1_3811C809A1E8494895518ACF57EFC9C2" purpose="section1"><h2>Interpret spatial lag model results</h2><p id="GUID-D058514A-D42D-4EBB-8581-B50C0FA3EFB3">The spatial lag model reports an additional coefficient called Lag Y (rho). This is the spatial lag of the dependent variable. The coefficient of this variable measures the strength and direction of spatial dependence of the dependent variable. The value of rho must be between -1 and 1. Larger values of Lag Y suggest a strong spatial feedback process.</p><p id="GUID-2F45CA36-8557-41AE-A1BE-7C5AFB04AD31"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Spatial lag model results summary table" title="Spatial lag model results summary table" src="GUID-8D1ABCC4-DD18-4CFC-86C9-75EDE40C9FFC-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-8BB171C2-0800-4DCC-88BE-6A6F001E825B">It is important to note that a change in an explanatory variable in one location can affect the value of the dependent variable in another location, called spatial spillover. In the presence of spatial spillover, the regression coefficients must be interpreted along with the spatial spillover effect. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:226: trailing whitespace.
+</p><section class="section2 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION2_9669C85093AE4BDEAC50472155CCF8A0" purpose="section2"><h3>Impacts and coefficient effects</h3><p id="GUID-7931AD3C-C687-40E0-85CD-B1D621743946">In addition to regression coefficients, a measure called impacts are reported. Impacts help measure the effect of spatial spillovers for each explanatory variable. They are broken down into direct, indirect, and total impacts. There are different approaches to calculating impacts, and this tool reports simple impacts.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:227: trailing whitespace.
+The direct, indirect, and total impacts are displayed in the Coefficient Effects Summary message table.</p><p id="GUID-E9BD8CE4-8D4D-4BE2-A42E-CE94CFB2182D"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Coefficient Effects Summary message table" title="Coefficient Effects Summary message table" src="GUID-A4208974-9126-431E-8D64-9D0570293645-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-446AFE88-D894-4DBA-9DFD-26C7DF82A2B4">The direct impact measures how much a one unit change in an explanatory variable affects the value of the dependent variable at the location itself. In the case of simple impacts, this is the same value as the beta coefficient. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:228: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-DB51EF55-B300-47BA-B728-460607B2FF9E"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Impact equations" title="Impact equations" src="GUID-57BF1E72-0B49-43F8-ABA8-B8D219361A91-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-12BF86BC-BCFF-4F25-AFD1-946E5F59C6E9">Whereas the indirect impact measures how much a one unit change in a variable affects the dependent variable in its neighboring locations. Note, however, that the value of impacts is strongly influenced by the spatial weights matrix.</p></section><section class="section2 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION2_3AB4ACA363A14EAD9EEDC37AD8419AD5" purpose="section2"><h3>Standard errors</h3><p id="GUID-1E983B71-1D17-4AB2-8116-CC54842156CB">By default, the spatial lag model reports robust standard errors. However, after fitting a spatial lag model, a large amount of autocorrelation in the residuals may remain. The Anselin-Kelejian (AK) test is a diagnostic test that is used to determine if a significant amount of spatial dependence remains in the model residuals. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:229: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-E0B02AE4-6345-4514-B44F-1D7BE8E09895"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Model Diagnostics message table" title="Model Diagnostics message table" src="GUID-F1B75C84-364A-4878-BA84-9199FFC40C78-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-5685249F-66A1-40EB-BA5B-733861566026">If the AK test is significant (p-value less than 0.05), a different measure of standard error, called heteroskedastic and autocorrelation robust (HAC) standard errors, are reported. HAC standard errors are a nonparametric variant of standard errors that are useful in the presence of spatial autocorrelation. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:230: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-69586D1E-D318-4404-938A-E249B76E3A64"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Spatial lag model results summary table" title="Spatial lag model results summary table" src="GUID-D445AA8E-7A1A-41B9-9988-A9ADB7E6DA8B-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-3A59227A-90C1-4699-B34A-E65B70F79B12">HAC standard errors take into account the spatial distribution of the data by using a separate spatial weights matrix. The spatial weights matrix is created using k nearest neighbors to identify each feature’s neighborhood with the focal feature included in the neighborhood. The weights of each neighborhood are modeled using a triangular kernel.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:231: trailing whitespace.
+</p></section><section class="section2 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION2_AA04451DED4E44DCBEC2C5AAED62CE3F" purpose="section2"><h3>Pseudo R-squared and spatial pseudo R-squared</h3><p id="GUID-14C9BEAA-12F4-4E91-B7C8-D1CF7751BD6B">Because the spatial lag model includes the spatial lag of the dependent variable as an explanatory variable, traditional linear regression prediction methods cannot be used. Predicting the dependent variable using its spatial lag leads to overconfident estimates. To overcome this, another measure called the spatial pseudo R-squared is calculated. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:232: trailing whitespace.
+
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:233: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-4CAA86A7-9891-489E-A5F9-3C239DF12BBC">The spatial pseudo R-squared is calculated without the spatial lag of the dependent variable. Instead, it uses the spatial weights matrix and the estimate of <span class="usertext">λ</span> to create a predicted values of <i>W<sub>y</sub>-hat</i> that is used in place of <i>W<sub>y</sub></i> in the prediction. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:234: trailing whitespace.
+
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:235: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-9DB99CE5-5CCE-4AA2-B75A-8640E5926C90">The predicted values are then used to calculate a traditional pseudo R-squared value. It is recommended that you report the spatial pseudo R-squared value over the pseudo R-squared value. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:236: trailing whitespace.
+
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:237: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-D28DE6D9-6021-40A8-ACDF-C26E7EE79B9C">It is important to note that the spatial pseudo R-squared is a different measure than the adjusted R-squared that is reported by OLS results. As such, it is inappropriate to compare the two.
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:238: trailing whitespace.
+</p></section></section><section class="section1 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION1_170CDF821C814F5F9D71B47C16A471B4" purpose="section1"><h2>Interpret spatial error model results</h2><p id="GUID-062BDAE3-6FE7-491C-90F9-28076E9C5AF9">In the spatial error model, the regression coefficients can be interpreted similarly to those in standard linear regression. Each coefficient represents the change in the dependent variable for a one-unit change in the independent variable. However, the SEM also includes an additional component, Lag Residual (lambda), which plays a crucial role in understanding spatial dependence within the model. The coefficient of Lag Residual (lambda) will always be between -0.99 and 0.99. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:239: trailing whitespace.
+</p><p id="GUID-3E0163FE-0A50-4515-8FD5-09BDDEE92B61"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Spatial error model results summary table" title="Spatial error model results summary table" src="GUID-A154918B-523E-4641-ADEC-4AF8FB11B571-web.png" loading="lazy"></p><p id="GUID-0D336B46-82D3-4AFD-9876-CBF73D128CF4">A positive value of lambda suggests that the residuals exhibit spatial clustering, and a negative value of lambda indicates that the residuals exhibit spatial dispersion. Larger absolute values (positive or negative) of lambda also suggest that there are spatial processes that are unaccounted for by the explanatory variables. Including additional relevant explanatory variables may reduce the coefficient to more moderate levels. 
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_method.html:240: trailing whitespace.
+</p></section><section class="section1 padded-anchor" id="ESRI_SECTION1_5593136BC31C47A3AF0DF7BC7C5F8060" purpose="section1"><h2>Interpret spatial autoregressive combined model results</h2><p id="GUID-A62BB786-92B9-46B9-AC27-79F1854C4129">When the SAC model is selected, all sections applicable to the SLM and the SEM models are displayed in the messages.</p><p id="GUID-AEBF5F3F-DB88-473B-B27F-D1E0F9DD2A2E"><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Summary of SAR Results" title="Summary of SAR Results" src="GUID-78CD9CD3-5FC8-4658-9459-5E390B75DE39-web.png" loading="lazy"></p></section><section class="section1 padded-anchor" id="GUID-6A7C1544-2618-4E21-AB1A-C004A2C21923" purpose="section1"><h2>References</h2><p id="GUID-76715CA8-7278-45B6-908A-75E6014D6F96">The following resources were used to implement the tool:<ul purpose="ul" id="UL_B005D9A5D0F54F1D86D2B073E352452A"><li purpose="li" id="LI_BC57E6252A824441A153875F97C6CB4B">Anselin, L., and  Sergio J. +<div class="section1 summary padded-anchor" id="S_GUID-1F55B80E-A248-43E4-A132-E296754F9D33" purpose="summary"><h2>Summary</h2><p id="GUID-1F55B80E-A248-43E4-A132-E296754F9D33">Estimates a global spatial regression model for a point or polygon feature class.</p><p id="GUID-F7C2A942-A04D-4503-B125-ACE0EB5DA8F1">The assumptions of traditional linear regression models are often violated when using spatial data. When spatial autocorrelation is present in a dataset, coefficient estimates may be biased and lead to overconfident inference. This tool can be used to estimate a regression model that is robust in the presence of spatial dependence and heteroskedasticity, as well as measure spatial spillovers. The tool uses Lagrange Multiplier (LM), also known as a Rao Score, diagnostic tests to determine the model that is most appropriate. Based on the LM diagnostics, either an ordinary least square (OLS), spatial lag model (SLM), spatial error model (SEM), or spatial autoregressive combined model (SAC) may be estimated.</p><p id="GUID-46CBB441-57C2-4435-A828-E9DA4F7C71A8"><a class="xref xref" href="/en/pro-app/3.5/tool-reference/spatial-statistics/how-spatial-autoregression-works.htm">Learn more about how Spatial Autoregression works</a></p></div>
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_tool.html:193: trailing whitespace.
+
docs/program/execution/2026-10-20/network-commercial-v1/acquisition/network/arcgis_tool.html:194: trailing whitespace.
+<div class="section1 illustration padded-anchor" id="I_ESRI_TOOLILLUSTRATION_19BD1BEAD9BA4FB18E583DFE1B3AC0B3" purpose="illustration"><h2>Illustration</h2><img purpose="img" placement="inline" alt="Spatial Autoregression tool illustration" title="Spatial Autoregression tool illustration" src="GUID-AF0B32A4-02E5-4813-B0D0-48E4F9A78400-web.png" loading="lazy"></div><div class="section1 gptoolusages padded-anchor" id="GUID-98FDF006-6F67-415C-8AAC-650EF4535CE2" purpose="gptoolusages"><h2>Usage</h2><ul purpose="gptoolusages"><li purpose="gptoolusage" cid="1HwXMa" id="ESRI_USAGE_959D1F8820744A6B9AB8E8E773BAB82B" esri_qref="no"><p id="GUID-8E111D78-D4B6-4EB4-AD49-5882A6A0E5CA">The tool accepts only point and polygon inputs.</p></li><li purpose="gptoolusage" id="ESRI_USAGE_9519754038CC450581B7D3C2995BF98B" esri_qref="no"><p id="GUID-7042359B-7B1C-495B-AF5C-2BA1AD13679A">The dependent variable must be continuous (not binary or categorical).</p></li><li purpose="gptoolusage" id="ESRI_USAGE_CE1BDC2BEF6E4A19BA89B4E80B112A8B" esri_qref="no"><p id="GUID-7B884A7C-176A-491E-BA2D-119C7CFBAE09">Explanatory variables must be continuous (not binary or categorical). Do no use binary variables (containing only the values 0 and 1, as they may violate model assumptions and cause an error.</p></li><li purpose="gptoolusage" id="ESRI_USAGE_814E09691F7C42BA8D1454B8541A85A9" esri_qref="no"><p id="GUID-38207393-EEF5-45BE-B893-200D573365AE">The output of the tool includes a Moran’s Scatter Plot of Residuals that can be used to identify autocorrelation in the model’s residuals.</p></li><li purpose="gptoolusage" id="ESRI_USAGE_F581B9893306451895FC3AE616E39025" esri_qref="no"><p id="GUID-1896A24A-D82D-41C8-A59F-AB27E13F4A9C">The spatial weights matrix used cannot have more than 30 percent connectivity. An error will occur if this threshold is reached to prevent biased estimates.</p></li><li purpose="gptoolusage" id="ESRI_USAGE_5E7D57C37AF342AA989C3893CBDB41E0" esri_qref="no"><p id="GUID-C87B215A-CF45-4EEF-AAD9-68E0D40D5DE0">When using k nearest neighbors with a local weighting scheme, an adaptive bandwidth will be calculated if no bandwidth is provided.</p></li><li purpose="gptoolusage" id="ESRI_USAGE_D6A9E307AB254377AB06D63546089072" esri_qref="no"><p id="GUID-7C5D7C39-4CF5-4BDE-B70A-1AACF07662E7">A Spatial Durbin model can be estimated by fitting a SLM and including each explanatory variable and their spatial lags. Use the <span purpose="gptoolref"><a class="xref xref" href="/en/pro-app/3.5/tool-reference/spatial-statistics/neighborhood-summary-statistics.htm">Neighborhood Summary Statistics</a></span> tool to calculate spatial lags.</p></li><li purpose="gptoolusage" id="ESRI_USAGE_93163B16FB254392903202FF47EEE2CC" esri_qref="no"><p id="GUID-74A83279-B703-43BA-81CF-C710AAF8F7A3">The models are estimated using the following methods related to heteroskedasticity and normality:<ul purpose="ul" id="UL_118266560B5949E6A08C5637C8AAB7A5"><li purpose="li" id="LI_6CD6DDB006074DC5802F6F9CF668C45C">SLM uses Spatial Two Stage Least Squares regression (S2SLS).</li><li purpose="li" id="LI_1FA7C23E5BEE466EABD83A7DD075D444">SEM uses Generalized Method of Moments (GMM).</li><li purpose="li" id="LI_94C63A4CFE0D4396ADF01AF8BC573035">SAC uses Generalized S2SLS (GS2SLS).</li></ul></p></li></ul></div><div class="section1 gpsyntax padded-anchor modifier-class trailer-2 js-tab-group" id="GUID-5A816A02-08BE-413A-81FC-E583C40FBE60" purpose="gptoolsyntax"><h2>Parameters</h2><nav class="tab-nav"><a class="tab-title is-active js-tab gptab" val="dialog">Dialog</a><a class="tab-title js-tab gptab" val="python">Python</a></nav><section class="tab-contents"><article class="tab-section js-tab-section is-active"><p><table class="gptoolparamtbl"><thead><tr><td class="th_p">Label</td><td class="th_e">Explanation</td><td class="th_d">Data Type</td></tr></thead><tr purpose="gptoolparamdialog" id="GUID-F5320E33-05B4-467A-9F1D-2F50EC04AA3A" paramname="in_features" expression="in_features"><td class="gptoolparamname"><div class="">Input Features

This is the default.</span><div></div></li><li><span>Spatial error model (SEM)</span>—<span>A SEM will be estimated regardless of the LM diagnostics.</span><div></div></li><li><span>Spatial lag model (SLM)</span>—<span>A SLM will be estimated  regardless of the LM diagnostics.</span><div></div></li><li><span>Spatial autoregressive combined (SAC)</span>—<span>A SAC will be estimated regardless of the LM diagnostics.</span><div></div></li></ul></div></td><td purpose="gptoolparamtype">String</td></tr><tr purpose="gptoolparamdialog" id="GUID-CAFFEA92-0AE1-461E-8FA7-D8865D562648" paramname="neighborhood_type" expression="neighborhood_type"><td class="gptoolparamname"><div class="">Neighborhood Type</div><div class="paramhint">(Optional)</div></td><td purpose="gptoolparamdesc"><p id="GUID-BA42AF8C-B367-49D6-AE92-0D4E47AA83BA"> Specifies how neighbors will be chosen for each input feature. To identify local spatial patterns, neighboring features must be identified for each input feature.</p><div class="enums" purpose="enums"><ul purpose="gptooldialogParamDesc"><li><span>Fixed distance band</span>—<span>Features within a specified distance of each feature will be considered neighbors. </span><div></div></li><li><span>K nearest neighbors</span>—<span>The closest k features will be considered neighbors. The number of neighbors is specified using the <span class="uicontrol">Number of Neighbors</span> parameter.</span><div></div></li><li><span>Contiguity edges only</span>—<span>Polygon features that share an edge will be included as neighbors.</span><div></div></li><li><span>Contiguity edges corners</span>—<span>Polygon features that share an edge or corner will be included as neighbors. This is the default for polygon features.</span><div></div></li><li><span>Delaunay triangulation</span>—<span>Features whose Delaunay triangulation share an edge or corner will be included as neighbors. This is the default for point features.</span><div></div></li><li><span>Get spatial weights from file</span>—<span>Neighbors and weights will be defined by a specified spatial weights file. id="GUID-3F210A48-EEE3-4576-AFE7-455E0D86E7C3"> The model type that will be used for the estimation. By default, LM diagnostic tests will be used to determine the model that is the most appropriate for the input data.</p><div class="enums" purpose="enums"><ul purpose="enums"><li purpose="enumrow"><span purpose="enumval">AUTO</span>—<span purpose="enumdesc">LM diagnostic tests will be used to determine whether an OLS, SLM, SEM, or SAC will be estimated. This is the default.</span><div></div></li><li purpose="enumrow"><span purpose="enumval">ERROR</span>—<span purpose="enumdesc">A SEM will be estimated regardless of the LM diagnostics.</span><div></div></li><li purpose="enumrow"><span purpose="enumval">LAG</span>—<span purpose="enumdesc">A SLM will be estimated  regardless of the LM diagnostics.</span><div></div></li><li purpose="enumrow"><span purpose="enumval">COMBINED</span>—<span purpose="enumdesc">A SAC will be estimated regardless of the LM diagnostics.</span><div></div></li></ul></div></td><td purpose="gptoolparamtype">String</td></tr><tr purpose="gptoolparam" id="GUID-CAFFEA92-0AE1-461E-8FA7-D8865D562648" paramname="neighborhood_type" expression="neighborhood_type"><td class="gptoolparamname"><div class="">neighborhood_type</div><div class="paramhint">(Optional)</div></td><td purpose="gptoolparamdesc"><p id="GUID-7FAFD159-99C0-475F-93AD-5573EE0E5CF1"> Specifies how neighbors will be chosen for each input feature. To identify local spatial patterns, neighboring features must be identified for each input feature.</p><div class="enums" purpose="enums"><ul purpose="enums"><li purpose="enumrow"><span purpose="enumval">DISTANCE_BAND</span>—<span purpose="enumdesc">Features within a specified distance of each feature will be considered neighbors. </span><div></div></li><li purpose="enumrow"><span purpose="enumval">K_NEAREST_NEIGHBORS</span>—<span purpose="enumdesc">The closest k features will be considered neighbors.
